Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: MARC is an open-source, YAML-configured multi-agent framework for clinical reasoning that replaces monolithic prompts with role-specialized agents, plus a Decomposer for automatic prompt generation, enabling traceable intermediate outputs and stage-wise failure attribution, with local CPU and API deployments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address fragility and opacity of single-prompt LLM reasoning, reduce manual prompt engineering, and improve deployability and interpretability for clinical use.

Method: Builds four role-specific agents (extraction, reasoning, answer-generation, evaluation) with explicit context passing and traceable outputs; introduces a Decomposer to generate task-specific prompts from plain-language descriptions; supports API-based and local CPU deployments; fully configurable via YAML with no code modification; model-agnostic.

Result: Deterministic orchestration with traceability and failure attribution across reasoning stages; enables easy deployment (CPU/API) and clinician accessibility; open-source release MARC-v1 at GitHub.

Conclusion: MARC is model-agnostic, interpretable, and accessible to clinical domain experts, providing a configurable, end-to-end reasoning pipeline for clinical tasks.

Abstract: We present Multi-Agent Reasoning and Coordination (MARC), an open-source framework that replaces monolithic LLM prompting with deterministic multi-agent orchestration for clinical reasoning. MARC coordinates role-specialized agents for extraction, reasoning, answer generation, and evaluation, with explicit context passing and traceable intermediate outputs, enabling stage-wise failure attribution. We additionally introduce a Decomposer module that generates task-specific agent prompts from a plain-language description, eliminating manual prompt engineering. The framework supports both API-based and local CPU-compatible deployments and is entirely configurable via YAML, without code modifications. MARC is designed to be model-agnostic, interpretable, and accessible to clinical domain experts without programming expertise. The full framework is available at https://github.com/Penn-RAIL/MARC-v1.

</details>


### [293] [AlayaWorld: Interactive Long-Horizon World Modeling - Full Technical Report (v1.1)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13492)
*AlayaWorld Team,Kaipeng Zhang,Chuanhao Li,Yifan Zhan,Yongtao Ge,Yuanyang Yin,Jiaming Tan,Kang He,Liaoyuan Fan,Mingliang Zhai,Ruicong Liu,Xiaojie Xu,Xuangeng Chu,Zhen Li,Zhengyuan Lin,Zhixiang Wang,Zian Meng,Zihui Gao*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Six design changes in AlayaWorld to align conditioning with generated video: motion-aware latent conditioning, streaming 3D point-cache renderer, temporally consistent causal-VAE encoding, memory-token dropout, unified VAE protocol across training/inference, and removal of camera AdaLN for view control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Improve conditioning fidelity by ensuring conditioning signals match generated content in both latent representation and temporal structure, addressing misalignment in prior conditioning.

Method: Replace depth-warping spatial memory with a streaming 3D point-cache renderer; encode visual conditions in the causal-VAE latent space with temporal statistics aligned to generated video; six modifications: (1) motion-aware latent conditioning; (2) causal encoding of re-rendered spatial memory as a continuous sequence; (3) align temporal-memory window in pixel space; (4) hard memory dropout removing memory tokens; (5) unify VAE encoding/decoding across training and inference; (6) remove camera AdaLN, relying on re-rendered spatial condition for viewpoint control.

Result: Not stated in the abstract; the text outlines architectural changes and intended benefits rather than empirical results.

Conclusion: The work argues for conditioning signals that closely match generated content in both latent and temporal dimensions, implemented via six coordinated architectural and training-time modifications to AlayaWorld to improve controllability and fidelity.

Abstract: This report presents an improved version of AlayaWorld. While the backbone architecture, chunk-wise autoregressive generation scheme, and training data remain unchanged from the previous release, we substantially revise how conditioning signals are represented and integrated into the model. The new design is guided by a simple principle: conditioning signals should match the generated content as closely as possible in both latent representation and temporal structure. To this end, we make two major changes. First, we replace the previous depth-warping-based spatial memory with a streaming 3D point-cache renderer. Second, we redesign the conditioning pipeline so that visual conditions are encoded in the same causal-VAE latent space, with temporal statistics consistent with those of the generated video. Concretely, the new version introduces six modifications: (1) replacing static-frame image conditioning with motion-aware latent conditioning; (2) causally encoding re-rendered spatial memory as a continuous sequence; (3) aligning the temporal-memory window in pixel space; (4) adopting hard memory dropout that removes memory tokens rather than zeroing them; (5) unifying the VAE encoding and decoding protocol across training and inference; and (6) removing the camera AdaLN branch, such that viewpoint control is provided entirely through the re-rendered spatial condition.

</details>


### [294] [QuoteBench: How Matched Scores Can Hide Command-Path Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13547)
*Shangao Li,Yao Zhang,Volker Tresp,Yuanyuan Yang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Final-state validation reveals the boundary between generation and execution in LLM command-issuing agents; QuoteBench demonstrates large deltas when replaying through an added parser, showing that matched scores misrepresent reliability; comprehensive reporting of configuration and boundary metrics is required.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the mismatch between surface-level matched execution scores and the underlying boundary between model-generated commands and post-generation execution behavior in interfaces that wrap or parse LLM outputs.

Method: 56 one-shot tasks drawn from 14 incident-derived families; exact final-state validation with an added deliberately unescaped parser; eight same-window configurations; replaying the same reply through the added parser; analysis of escaping at the interpolation point to reproduce raw-path outcomes; evaluation across different model configurations and deployment settings to separate generation from execution effects.

Result: Replaying the same reply through the added parser reduces success by 55.4 to 73.2 percentage points across eight configurations; disclosure recovers 30.4 to 60.7 points for six configurations, and zero or slightly negative for the other two; GPT-5.6-sol’s matched gap of -3.6 points masks -64.3 points of damage and +60.7 points of compensation; the deployment configuration reorders models with one unambiguous reversal among 26 comparable pairs; four additional single-task margins exist; raw generation is near saturation while boundary adaptation differentiates models.

Conclusion: Evaluations of command-issuing agents should report model configuration, generation contract, execution path, operating point, and final-state validator rather than rely on a single matched score; boundary-aware evaluation is essential for understanding true reliability and deployment behavior.

Abstract: LLM coding agents issue Bash commands through interfaces that may serialize, wrap, and reparse model output. Matched execution scores alone cannot distinguish command-generation errors from failures introduced after generation. QuoteBench measures this boundary with exact final-state validation on 56 one-shot tasks from 14 incident-derived families, crossing the generation contract with the execution transport around one deliberately unescaped added parser. Escaping at the interpolation point reproduces each replayed reply's raw-path outcome, so any recovery under a disclosed boundary must come from the model changing its generation. Across eight same-window configurations, replaying the same reply through the added parser lowers success by 55.4 to 73.2 percentage points; disclosure recovers 30.4 to 60.7 points for six configurations, and zero or slightly negative for the other two. Raw generation is nearly saturated at the frontier; boundary adaptation is what still separates models. GPT-5.6-sol's matched gap of -3.6 points hides -64.3 points of damage and +60.7 points of compensation. The deployment configuration reorders models: one reversal among 26 comparable pairs is unambiguous and four more sit on single-task margins. Evaluations of command-issuing agents should report the model configuration, generation contract, execution path, operating point, and final-state validator rather than treat a matched score as an intrinsic model property.

</details>


### [295] [OmniScientist: An Omni-Modal Omni-Discipline AI Scientist](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13558)
*Bobo Li,Hao Fei,Tianjie Ju,Mong-Li Lee,Wynne Hsu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: OmniScientist demonstrates lifecycle-wide, omni-modal AI scientific reasoning from raw data to manuscript using a perception layer and three autonomous agents (ideation, experiment, writeup) that operate in a deterministic pipeline, achieving full-data-to-paper automation and outperforming a precomputed-feature baseline.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current AI systems primarily reason over text, code, or precomputed summaries and miss decisive spatial, temporal, cross-modal, and procedural relations essential for evidence-based discovery.

Method: Introduce a perception layer and three autonomous agents for ideation, experimentation, and writeup within a deterministic pipeline. Observations influence questions, experimental decisions, and final claims. Enforce novelty, rigor, and provenance through code-level checks (idea, rigour, claim). Evaluate on 36 real-data cases across 5 discipline families, 4 evidence families, and modalities including images, signals, audio, video, 3-D structures, trajectories, tables, formulae, and graphs.

Result: OmniScientist completes the full path from raw data to a compiled manuscript in all 36 cases; mean overall paper score 6.3 with the reference reasoning backbone. In a blind comparison against a precomputed-scalar-feature variant, direct perception improves all 7 evaluation dimensions and wins 85% of head-to-head judgments.

Conclusion: Lifecycle-wide perception is essential for evidence-grounded scientific discovery and provides a practical path toward broadly capable AI scientists.

Abstract: Recent advances in foundation models have enabled AI scientists to automate increasingly complete research workflows, from hypothesis generation and code execution to manuscript preparation. Yet workflow coverage alone does not provide access to the full evidence on which scientific discovery depends. Existing systems typically reason over text, code, labels, or precomputed summaries, leaving scientifically decisive spatial, temporal, cross-channel, and procedural relations unavailable to the agent. We introduce OmniScientist, an end-to-end, omni-modal AI scientist that conducts multidisciplinary research directly from heterogeneous raw evidence. A perception layer and 3 autonomous agents for ideation, experiment, and writeup operate within a deterministic pipeline, allowing observations to shape research questions, experimental decisions, and final claims throughout the research lifecycle. By running idea, rigour, and claim checks in code, the system enforces novelty screening, statistical validity, execution provenance, and numerical traceability. We evaluate OmniScientist on 36 real-data cases spanning 5 discipline families, 4 families of scientific evidence, and modalities including images, signals, audio, video, 3-D structures, trajectories, tables, formulae, and graphs. The system completes the full path from raw data to a compiled manuscript in all 36 cases and achieves a mean overall paper score of 6.3 with the reference reasoning backbone. In paired comparisons against a blind variant that receives only precomputed scalar features, direct perception improves all 7 evaluation dimensions and wins 85% of head-to-head judgments. These results show that lifecycle-wide perception is essential for evidence-grounded scientific discovery and provides a practical path toward broadly capable AI scientists.

</details>
